If someone slowed me down or questioned what I was doing, I’d snap at them harder than I meant to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, then wake up before it was even light out feeling like I was ready to start the day.\n\nI’d be up making plans while the house was still dark, and at the time it didn’t seem strange to me. I didn’t really feel tired like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you heard voices or felt certain of things that other people said weren’t true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those times, I did. I was hearing calls that weren’t really there, like someone calling out or trying to get my attention, but nobody had said anything.\n\nAnd I got really sure my supervisor was setting traps for me at work. Other people told me that didn’t make sense, but at the time I felt certain about it.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices or beliefs about your supervisor happen only when you were feeling unusually up and energetic?\n\nPatient: No, not only then. There was a calmer stretch too, where I was sleeping more normal and I wasn’t rushing around doing projects.\n\nBut even then, for several weeks, I’d still hear my name being called when nobody was there. And I still felt pretty sure people at work were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a time when you felt very depressed and, during that time, still heard voices or felt people were watching or plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I’ve had times I felt pretty low, but I can’t clearly say the voices or the feeling watched was tied to that the way you’re asking.\n\nI’d have to think more on it.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing voices or felt people were plotting against you, but you were not feeling especially up or especially depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. There was a stretch like that. I wasn’t feeling real up or wired, and I wasn’t especially depressed either. I was sleeping more normal, not running around starting projects.\n\nBut for several weeks I was still hearing my name being called when nobody was there, and I still felt like people at work were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve had low periods, yeah, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day for two weeks straight. I didn’t really track it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt down or depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I still can’t say for sure. I’ve had times where I was down for a while, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day for a full two weeks. I wasn’t keeping track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know. I’ve had times where I felt pretty down, but I can’t honestly say it was nearly every day for two weeks or longer. I wasn’t paying that close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know I’ve had stretches where I felt low, but I can’t say it was nearly every day for two weeks. I just wasn’t keeping track like that.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I’ve had times where I felt down a lot, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day nearly every day. I didn’t really track it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, but I can’t say I lost interest in almost everything for two weeks or longer. I don’t really remember it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the whole time you’ve been unwell, have the voices or suspicious beliefs been present for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. The suspicious thoughts and the hearing calls that aren’t really there have been going on for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, was there ever a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had low periods during that time, but I can’t say it was nearly every day for two weeks straight. I don’t remember it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: During these nine months, have there also been long stretches when you were not feeling especially up or depressed, but the voices or suspicious beliefs were still there?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There was a calmer stretch where I wasn’t really up or down. I was sleeping more normal, not running around trying to do projects or anything. But even then, for several weeks, I’d still hear my name being called, and I still thought people at work were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt extremely energized or high for at least one week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’ve had stretches like that. I feel really bright and fired up, like everything’s finally coming together. But if somebody slows me down or questions what I’m doing, I can snap pretty hard, harder than I mean to.\n\nDoctor: When you were hearing your name being called during that calmer stretch, were you feeling completely back to your usual self emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was more like myself mood-wise, I guess. I wasn’t all revved up or really down, and my sleep was more normal. But the voices were still happening for weeks, hearing my name, and I still thought people at work were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Over these nine months, have the times when your mood was clearly high or energized lasted for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Looking back, the high-energy times — where I had all these big plans and got irritated real easy — they took up more of the nine months than the calmer stretches did.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
